		failAt(ctx, where, value.F0)
	}
	panic("unreachable")
}

func calDateNewBang(ctx *actor.Context, year, month, day int64, where string) TDate {
	return calOk[TDate](ctx, calDateNew(year, month, day), where)
}
func calTimeNewBang(ctx *actor.Context, hour, minute, second int64, where string) TTime {
	return calOk[TTime](ctx, calTimeNew(hour, minute, second), where)
}
func calNaiveNewBang(ctx *actor.Context, year, month, day, hour, minute, second int64, where string) TNaiveDateTime {
	return calOk[TNaiveDateTime](ctx, calNaiveNew(year, month, day, hour, minute, second), where)
}
func calNaiveJoinBang(ctx *actor.Context, d TDate, t TTime, where string) TNaiveDateTime {
	return calOk[TNaiveDateTime](ctx, calNaiveJoin(d, t), where)
}

func calDateToday() TDate         { return calDateOf(time.Now().UTC()) }
func calTimeNow() TTime           { return calTimeOf(time.Now().UTC()) }
func calNaiveNow() TNaiveDateTime { return calNaiveOf(time.Now().UTC()) }
func calDateTimeNow() TDateTime   { return calDateTimeOf(time.Now()) }

// calParse reads ISO 8601 text with the first layout that fits.
func calParse(text string, layouts ...string) (time.Time, bool) {
	for _, layout := range layouts {
		if moment, err := time.Parse(layout, text); err == nil {
			return moment, true
		}
	}
	return time.Time{}, false
}

func calDateParse(text string) Tresult[TDate, string] {
	moment, ok := calParse(text, "2006-01-02")
	if !ok {
		return errOf[TDate]("invalid date: " + strconv.Quote(text))
	}
	return okOf(calDateOf(moment))
}
func calTimeParse(text string) Tresult[TTime, string] {
	moment, ok := calParse(text, "15:04:05")
	if !ok {
		return errOf[TTime]("invalid time: " + strconv.Quote(text))
	}
	return okOf(calTimeOf(moment))
}
func calNaiveParse(text string) Tresult[TNaiveDateTime, string] {
	moment, ok := calParse(text, "2006-01-02T15:04:05", "2006-01-02 15:04:05")
	if !ok {
		return errOf[TNaiveDateTime]("invalid date and time: " + strconv.Quote(text))
	}
	return okOf(calNaiveOf(moment))
}

// A DateTime is kept in UTC; the offset the text carried is returned beside it.
func calDateTimeParse(text string) Tresult[pair[TDateTime, int64], string] {
	moment, ok := calParse(text, time.RFC3339, "2006-01-02 15:04:05Z07:00")
	if !ok {
		return errOf[pair[TDateTime, int64]]("invalid date and time with offset: " + strconv.Quote(text))
	}
	_, offset := moment.Zone()
	return okOf(pair[TDateTime, int64]{calDateTimeOf(moment), int64(offset)})
}
func calDateParseBang(ctx *actor.Context, text string, where string) TDate {
	return calOk[TDate](ctx, calDateParse(text), where)
}
func calTimeParseBang(ctx *actor.Context, text string, where string) TTime {
	return calOk[TTime](ctx, calTimeParse(text), where)
}
func calNaiveParseBang(ctx *actor.Context, text string, where string) TNaiveDateTime {
	return calOk[TNaiveDateTime](ctx, calNaiveParse(text), where)
}

// day_of_week is 1 for Monday through 7 for Sunday.
func calDayOfWeek(d TDate) int64 { return (int64(calFromDate(d).Weekday())+6)%7 + 1 }
func calDayOfYear(d TDate) int64 { return int64(calFromDate(d).YearDay()) }
func calDaysInMonth(d TDate) int64 {
	return int64(calMoment(d.F0, d.F1+1, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0).Day())
}
func calLeapYear(d TDate) bool  { return calMoment(d.F0, 2, 29, 0, 0, 0, 0).Month() == time.February }
func calQuarter(d TDate) int64  { return (d.F1-1)/3 + 1 }
func calMonthStart(d TDate) TDate { return TDate{d.F0, d.F1, 1} }
func calMonthEnd(d TDate) TDate   { return TDate{d.F0, d.F1, calDaysInMonth(d)} }
func calWeekStart(d TDate) TDate {
	return calDateOf(calFromDate(d).AddDate(0, 0, -int(calDayOfWeek(d)-1)))
}
func calWeekEnd(d TDate) TDate {
	return calDateOf(calFromDate(d).AddDate(0, 0, int(7-calDayOfWeek(d))))
}

func calNaiveDate(n TNaiveDateTime) TDate { return TDate{n.F0, n.F1, n.F2} }
func calNaiveTime(n TNaiveDateTime) TTime { return TTime{n.F3, n.F4, n.F5, n.F6} }
func calToNaive(d TDateTime) TNaiveDateTime {
	return TNaiveDateTime{d.F0, d.F1, d.F2, d.F3, d.F4, d.F5, d.F6}
}
func calDateTimeDate(d TDateTime) TDate { return TDate{d.F0, d.F1, d.F2} }
func calDateTimeTime(d TDateTime) TTime { return TTime{d.F3, d.F4, d.F5, d.F6} }
func calFromNaive(ctx *actor.Context, n TNaiveDateTime, where string) TDateTime {
	if !calValid(n.F0, n.F1, n.F2, n.F3, n.F4, n.F5, n.F6) {
		failAt(ctx, where, "invalid date or time")
	}
	return TDateTime{n.F0, n.F1, n.F2, n.F3, n.F4, n.F5, n.F6, "Etc/UTC", 0}
}

func calFromUnix(seconds int64) Tresult[TDateTime, string] {
	if seconds < -377705116800 || seconds > 253402300799 {
		return errOf[TDateTime]("the Unix time is outside years -9999 to 9999")
	}
	return okOf(calDateTimeOf(time.Unix(seconds, 0)))
}
func calFromUnixBang(ctx *actor.Context, seconds int64, where string) TDateTime {
	return calOk[TDateTime](ctx, calFromUnix(seconds), where)
}
func calToUnix(d TDateTime) int64 { return calFromDateTime(d).Unix() }

// strftime formats a date or time with %-directives. A directive may carry
// a padding flag (- for none, 0 for zeros, _ for spaces) and a width.
func calStrftime[A any](ctx *actor.Context, value A, format string, where string) string {
	var at time.Time
	hasDate, hasTime, micro, zone := false, false, int64(0), false
	offset, abbreviation := int64(0), "UTC"
	switch v := any(value).(type) {
	case TDate:
		at, hasDate = calFromDate(v), true
	case TTime:
		at, hasTime, micro = calMoment(1970, 1, 1, v.F0, v.F1, v.F2, 0), true, v.F3
	case TNaiveDateTime:
		at, hasDate, hasTime, micro = calMoment(v.F0, v.F1, v.F2, v.F3, v.F4, v.F5, 0), true, true, v.F6
	case TDateTime:
		at, hasDate, hasTime, micro, zone = calMoment(v.F0, v.F1, v.F2, v.F3, v.F4, v.F5, 0), true, true, v.F6, true
		offset = v.F8
		if place, ok := calZone(v.F7); ok && place != time.UTC {
			abbreviation, _ = calFromDateTime(v).In(place).Zone()
		}
	}
	var out strings.Builder
	for i := 0; i < len(format); i++ {
		if format[i] != '%' {
			out.WriteByte(format[i])
			continue
		}
		i++
		pad := byte(0)
		if i < len(format) && (format[i] == '-' || format[i] == '0' || format[i] == '_') {
			pad = format[i]
			i++
		}
		width := -1
		for i < len(format) && format[i] >= '0' && format[i] <= '9' {
			width = max(width, 0)*10 + int(format[i]-'0')
			i++
		}
		if i >= len(format) {
			failAt(ctx, where, "strftime format ends in the middle of a directive")
		}
		directive := format[i]
		number := func(n int64, digits int, fill byte) {
			text := strconv.FormatInt(n, 10)
			if width >= 0 {
				digits = width
			}
			switch pad {
			case '-':
				digits = 0
			case '0':
				fill = '0'
			case '_':
				fill = ' '
			}
			for n := len(text); n < digits; n++ {
				out.WriteByte(fill)
			}
			out.WriteString(text)
		}
		text := func(value string) {
			if pad == '-' {
				width = 0
			}
			for n := len(value); n < width; n++ {
				out.WriteByte(' ')
			}
			out.WriteString(value)
		}
		needs := func(present bool, what string) {
			if !present {
				failAt(ctx, where, "strftime: %"+string(directive)+" needs a value with a "+what)
			}
		}
		switch directive {
		case '%':
			out.WriteByte('%')
		case 'Y':
			needs(hasDate, "date")
			number(int64(at.Year()), 0, '0')
		case 'y':
			needs(hasDate, "date")
			number(int64(at.Year()%100), 2, '0')
		case 'm':
			needs(hasDate, "date")
			number(int64(at.Month()), 2, '0')
		case 'd':
			needs(hasDate, "date")
			number(int64(at.Day()), 2, '0')
		case 'j':
			needs(hasDate, "date")
			number(int64(at.YearDay()), 3, '0')
		case 'q':
			needs(hasDate, "date")
			number(int64((at.Month()-1)/3+1), 1, '0')
		case 'u':
			needs(hasDate, "date")
			number((int64(at.Weekday())+6)%7+1, 1, '0')
		case 'a':
			needs(hasDate, "date")
			text(at.Weekday().String()[:3])
		case 'A':
			needs(hasDate, "date")
			text(at.Weekday().String())
		case 'b':
			needs(hasDate, "date")
			text(at.Month().String()[:3])
		case 'B':
			needs(hasDate, "date")
			text(at.Month().String())
		case 'H':
			needs(hasTime, "time")
			number(int64(at.Hour()), 2, '0')
		case 'I':
			needs(hasTime, "time")
			number(int64((at.Hour()+11)%12+1), 2, '0')
		case 'M':
			needs(hasTime, "time")
			number(int64(at.Minute()), 2, '0')
		case 'S':
			needs(hasTime, "time")
			number(int64(at.Second()), 2, '0')
		case 'f':
			needs(hasTime, "time")
			if micro == 0 {
				out.WriteByte('0')
			} else {
				out.WriteString(strings.TrimRight(fmt.Sprintf("%06d", micro), "0"))
			}
		case 'p', 'P':
			needs(hasTime, "time")
			half := "am"
			if at.Hour() >= 12 {
				half = "pm"
			}
			if directive == 'p' {
				half = strings.ToUpper(half)
			}
			text(half)
		case 'z':
			needs(zone, "time zone")
			text(calOffset(offset, ""))
		case 'Z':
			needs(zone, "time zone")
			text(abbreviation)
		case 'c':
			needs(hasDate && hasTime, "date and time")
			out.WriteString(at.Format("2006-01-02 15:04:05"))
		case 'x':
			needs(hasDate, "date")
			out.WriteString(at.Format("2006-01-02"))
		case 'X':
			needs(hasTime, "time")
			out.WriteString(at.Format("15:04:05"))
		default:
			failAt(ctx, where, "strftime: unknown directive %"+string(directive))
		}
	}
	return out.String()
}

// A Duration holds an amount per unit; nothing is carried between units, so
// one month and thirty days stay different.
func durationOf(index int, amount int64) TDuration {
	var parts [8]int64
	parts[index] = amount
	return TDuration{parts[0], parts[1], parts[2], parts[3], parts[4], parts[5], parts[6], parts[7]}
}

func durationUnit0(n int64) TDuration { return durationOf(0, n) }
func durationUnit1(n int64) TDuration { return durationOf(1, n) }
func durationUnit2(n int64) TDuration { return durationOf(2, n) }
func durationUnit3(n int64) TDuration { return durationOf(3, n) }
func durationUnit4(n int64) TDuration { return durationOf(4, n) }
func durationUnit5(n int64) TDuration { return durationOf(5, n) }
func durationUnit6(n int64) TDuration { return durationOf(6, n) }
func durationUnit7(n int64) TDuration { return durationOf(7, n) }

func durationAdd(a, b TDuration) TDuration {
	return TDuration{a.F0 + b.F0, a.F1 + b.F1, a.F2 + b.F2, a.F3 + b.F3, a.F4 + b.F4, a.F5 + b.F5, a.F6 + b.F6, a.F7 + b.F7}
}
func durationMultiply(d TDuration, n int64) TDuration {
	return TDuration{d.F0 * n, d.F1 * n, d.F2 * n, d.F3 * n, d.F4 * n, d.F5 * n, d.F6 * n, d.F7 * n}
}
func durationNegate(d TDuration) TDuration      { return durationMultiply(d, -1) }
func durationSubtract(a, b TDuration) TDuration { return durationAdd(a, durationNegate(b)) }

// durationIso writes P[n]Y[n]M[n]W[n]DT[n]H[n]M[n]S, leaving out zero units.
func durationIso(d TDuration) string {
	var out strings.Builder
	out.WriteByte('P')
	for _, part := range []struct {
		amount int64
		unit   byte
	}{{d.F0, 'Y'}, {d.F1, 'M'}, {d.F2, 'W'}, {d.F3, 'D'}} {
		if part.amount != 0 {
			out.WriteString(strconv.FormatInt(part.amount, 10))
			out.WriteByte(part.unit)
		}
	}
	var clock strings.Builder
	if d.F4 != 0 {
		clock.WriteString(strconv.FormatInt(d.F4, 10) + "H")
	}
	if d.F5 != 0 {
		clock.WriteString(strconv.FormatInt(d.F5, 10) + "M")
	}
	if d.F6 != 0 || d.F7 != 0 {
		// Seconds and microseconds are written as one number.
		total := d.F6*1000000 + d.F7
		sign := ""
		if total < 0 {
			sign, total = "-", -total
		}
		clock.WriteString(sign + strconv.FormatInt(total/1000000, 10) + calFraction(total%1000000) + "S")
	}
	if clock.Len() > 0 {
		out.WriteString("T" + clock.String())
	}
	if out.Len() == 1 {
		return "PT0S"
	}
	return out.String()
}

func durationParse(text string) Tresult[TDuration, string] {
	bad := errOf[TDuration]("invalid duration " + strconv.Quote(text))
	if len(text) < 3 || text[0] != 'P' {
		return bad
	}
	var d TDuration
	inClock, seen := false, false
	for rest := text[1:]; rest != ""; {
		if rest[0] == 'T' {
			if inClock {
				return bad
			}
			inClock, rest = true, rest[1:]
			continue
		}
		end := 0
		for end < len(rest) && (rest[end] == '-' || rest[end] == '.' || rest[end] >= '0' && rest[end] <= '9') {
			end++
		}
		if end == 0 || end == len(rest) {
			return bad
		}
		number, unit := rest[:end], rest[end]
		rest = rest[end+1:]
		if inClock && unit == 'S' {
			seconds, err := strconv.ParseFloat(number, 64)
			if err != nil {
				return bad
			}
			micros := int64(math.Round(seconds * 1000000))
			d.F6, d.F7 = micros/1000000, micros%1000000
			seen = true
			continue
		}
		amount, err := strconv.ParseInt(number, 10, 64)
		if err != nil {
			return bad
		}
		switch {
		case !inClock && unit == 'Y':
			d.F0 = amount
		case !inClock && unit == 'M':
			d.F1 = amount
		case !inClock && unit == 'W':
			d.F2 = amount
		case !inClock && unit == 'D':
			d.F3 = amount
		case inClock && unit == 'H':
			d.F4 = amount
		case inClock && unit == 'M':
			d.F5 = amount
		default:
			return bad
		}
		seen = true
	}
	if !seen {
		return bad
	}
	return okOf(d)
}

// calShiftWall moves a wall clock by a duration: years and months first,
// keeping the day of the month or the last day the new month has, then
// weeks and days, then the time units.
func calShiftWall(ctx *actor.Context, t time.Time, d TDuration, where string) time.Time {
	for _, amount := range []int64{d.F0, d.F1, d.F2, d.F3, d.F4, d.F5, d.F6, d.F7} {
		if amount > 1<<40 || amount < -(1<<40) {
			failAt(ctx, where, "the duration is out of range")
		}
	}
	months := d.F0*12 + d.F1
	if months != 0 {
		day := t.Day()
		first := time.Date(t.Year(), t.Month()+time.Month(months), 1, t.Hour(), t.Minute(), t.Second(), t.Nanosecond(), t.Location())
		last := first.AddDate(0, 1, -1).Day()
		t = first.AddDate(0, 0, min(day, last)-1)
	}
	t = t.AddDate(0, 0, int(d.F2*7+d.F3))
	return t.Add(time.Duration(d.F4)*time.Hour + time.Duration(d.F5)*time.Minute + time.Duration(d.F6)*time.Second + time.Duration(d.F7)*time.Microsecond)
}

func calDateShift(ctx *actor.Context, date TDate, d TDuration, where string) TDate {
	if d.F4 != 0 || d.F5 != 0 || d.F6 != 0 || d.F7 != 0 {
		failAt(ctx, where, "a date is shifted by years, months, weeks and days only")
	}
	return calDateOf(calShiftWall(ctx, calFromDate(date), d, where))
}

func calTimeShift(ctx *actor.Context, t TTime, d TDuration, where string) TTime {
	if d.F0 != 0 || d.F1 != 0 || d.F2 != 0 || d.F3 != 0 {
		failAt(ctx, where, "a time is shifted by hours, minutes, seconds and microseconds only")
	}
	return calTimeOf(calShiftWall(ctx, calMoment(1970, 1, 1, t.F0, t.F1, t.F2, t.F3), d, where))
}

func calNaiveDateTimeShift(ctx *actor.Context, n TNaiveDateTime, d TDuration, where string) TNaiveDateTime {
	return calNaiveOf(calShiftWall(ctx, calFromNaiveTime(n), d, where))
}

// A DateTime is shifted on its own wall clock for the calendar units and by
// elapsed time for the rest, and stays in its zone.
func calDateTimeShift(ctx *actor.Context, value TDateTime, d TDuration, where string) TDateTime {
	zone, ok := calZone(value.F7)
	if !ok {
		zone = time.UTC
	}
	calendar := TDuration{d.F0, d.F1, d.F2, d.F3, 0, 0, 0, 0}
	clock := TDuration{0, 0, 0, 0, d.F4, d.F5, d.F6, d.F7}
	wall := calShiftWall(ctx, calFromDateTime(value).In(zone), calendar, where)
	return calKeepZone(calShiftWall(ctx, wall, clock, where), value)
}

// ---- URI and Version ----

// uriEncode escapes every byte that is neither reserved nor unreserved in a
// URI, so an address keeps its structure and a space becomes %20.
func uriEncode(text string) string {
	const keep = "-._~:/?#[]@!$&'()*+,;="
	var out strings.Builder
	for i := 0; i < len(text); i++ {
		c := text[i]
		if c >= 'a' && c <= 'z' || c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z' || c >= '0' && c <= '9' || strings.IndexByte(keep, c) >= 0 {
			out.WriteByte(c)
		} else {
			fmt.Fprintf(&out, "%%%02X", c)
		}
	}
	return out.String()
}

func uriEncodeForm(text string) string { return url.QueryEscape(text) }

func uriDecode(text string) Tresult[string, string] {
	decoded, err := url.PathUnescape(text)
	if err != nil {
		return errOf[string]("malformed URI " + strconv.Quote(text))
	}
	return okOf(decoded)
}

func uriDecodeForm(text string) Tresult[string, string] {
	decoded, err := url.QueryUnescape(text)
	if err != nil {
		return errOf[string]("malformed URI " + strconv.Quote(text))
	}
	return okOf(decoded)
}

func uriEncodeQuery(fields actor.Map[string, string]) string {
	keys, values := actor.MapSorted(fields, nil)
	parts := make([]string, len(keys))
	for i, key := range keys {
		parts[i] = url.QueryEscape(key) + "=" + url.QueryEscape(values[i])
	}
	return strings.Join(parts, "&")
}

// decode_query reads key=value pairs separated by &. A later pair replaces
// an earlier one with the same key, and a pair that cannot be decoded is
// kept as written.
func uriDecodeQuery(query string) actor.Map[string, string] {
	out := actor.NewMapBuilder(actor.Map[string, string]{})
	for _, part := range strings.Split(query, "&") {
		if part == "" {
			continue
		}
		key, value, _ := strings.Cut(part, "=")
		if decoded, err := url.QueryUnescape(key); err == nil {
			key = decoded
		}
		if decoded, err := url.QueryUnescape(value); err == nil {
			value = decoded
		}
		out.Put(key, value)
	}
	return out.Map()
}

// versionParts reads MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH with an optional -pre-release and
// +build, which is ignored.
func versionParts(text string) (numbers [3]int64, pre string, ok bool) {
	text, _, _ = strings.Cut(text, "+")
	core, pre, hasPre := strings.Cut(text, "-")
	if hasPre && pre == "" {
		return numbers, "", false
	}
	fields := strings.Split(core, ".")
	if len(fields) != 3 {
		return numbers, "", false
	}
	for i, field := range fields {
		n, err := strconv.ParseInt(field, 10, 64)
		if err != nil || n < 0 || (len(field) > 1 && field[0] == '0') {
			return numbers, "", false
		}
		numbers[i] = n
	}
	return numbers, pre, true
}

func versionParse(text string) Tresult[TVersion, string] {
	numbers, pre, ok := versionParts(text)
	if !ok {
		return errOf[TVersion]("invalid version " + strconv.Quote(text))
	}
	v := TVersion{numbers[0], numbers[1], numbers[2], mk([]string{}), noneOf[string]()}
	if pre != "" {
		v.F3 = mk(strings.Split(pre, "."))
	}
	if _, build, found := strings.Cut(text, "+"); found {
		v.F4 = someOf(build)
	}
	return okOf(v)
}

func versionParseBang(ctx *actor.Context, text string, where string) TVersion {
	parsed := versionParse(text)
	if v, ok := parsed.(Tresult_c0[TVersion, string]); ok {
		return v.F0
	}
	failAt(ctx, where, errorOf[TVersion, string](parsed))
	return TVersion{}
}

// versionString writes a version back as text, with its pre-release and
// build.
func versionString(v TVersion) string {
	text := fmt.Sprintf("%d.%d.%d", v.F0, v.F1, v.F2)
	if pre := fwd(v.F3); len(pre) > 0 {
		text += "-" + strings.Join(pre, ".")
	}
	if build, ok := v.F4.(Toption_c1[string]); ok {
		text += "+" + build.F0
	}
	return text
}

func versionCompareStructs(ctx *actor.Context, a, b TVersion, where string) actor.Atom {
	return versionCompareAtom(ctx, versionString(a), versionString(b), where)
}

// versionRequirement checks a requirement once, so that matching it later
// cannot fail.
func versionRequirement(ctx *actor.Context, text string, where string) Tresult[TVersion_oRequirement, string] {
	if !versionValidRequirement(text) {
		return errOf[TVersion_oRequirement]("invalid requirement " + strconv.Quote(text))
	}
	return okOf(TVersion_oRequirement{text})
}

func versionRequirementBang(ctx *actor.Context, text string, where string) TVersion_oRequirement {
	if !versionValidRequirement(text) {
		failAt(ctx, where, "invalid requirement "+strconv.Quote(text))
	}
	return TVersion_oRequirement{text}
}

// versionValidRequirement reports whether matching a valid version against
// the requirement succeeds rather than failing.
func versionValidRequirement(text string) (valid bool) {
	defer func() {
		if recover() != nil {
			valid = false
		}
	}()
	versionMatch(nil, "0.0.0", text, "")
	return true
}

func versionMatchStruct(ctx *actor.Context, v TVersion, requirement string, where string) bool {
	return versionMatch(ctx, versionString(v), requirement, where)
}
func versionMatchRequirement(ctx *actor.Context, v TVersion, requirement TVersion_oRequirement, where string) bool {
	return versionMatch(ctx, versionString(v), requirement.F0, where)
}
func versionMatchStringRequirement(ctx *actor.Context, v string, requirement TVersion_oRequirement, where string) bool {
	return versionMatch(ctx, v, requirement.F0, where)
}

// versionMatchOptions takes allow_pre: false, under which a pre-release
// matches only a requirement that names a pre-release.
func versionMatchOptions(ctx *actor.Context, v, requirement string, options L[pair[actor.Atom, bool]], where string) bool {
	for _, option := range fwd(options) {
		if option.E0 != "allow_pre" {
			failAt(ctx, where, "Version.match? takes allow_pre:")
		}
		if _, pre, ok := versionParts(v); ok && pre != "" && !option.E1 && !strings.Contains(requirement, "-") {
			versionMatch(ctx, "0.0.0", requirement, where)
			return false
		}
	}
	return versionMatch(ctx, v, requirement, where)
}

// versionCompare orders two versions: -1, 0 or 1. A pre-release sorts
// before its release, and pre-release identifiers compare numerically when
// both are numbers.
func versionCompareAtom(ctx *actor.Context, a, b string, where string) actor.Atom {
	return orderAtom(versionCompare(ctx, a, b, where))
}

func versionCompare(ctx *actor.Context, a, b string, where string) int64 {
	left, leftPre, ok := versionParts(a)
	if !ok {
		failAt(ctx, where, "invalid version "+strconv.Quote(a))
	}
	right, rightPre, ok := versionParts(b)
	if !ok {
		failAt(ctx, where, "invalid version "+strconv.Quote(b))
	}
	for i := range left {
		if left[i] != right[i] {
			if left[i] < right[i] {
				return -1
			}
			return 1
		}
	}
	switch {
	case leftPre == rightPre:
		return 0
	case leftPre == "":
		return 1
	case rightPre == "":
		return -1
	}
	leftIDs, rightIDs := strings.Split(leftPre, "."), strings.Split(rightPre, ".")
	for i := 0; i < len(leftIDs) && i < len(rightIDs); i++ {
		x, xErr := strconv.ParseInt(leftIDs[i], 10, 64)
		y, yErr := strconv.ParseInt(rightIDs[i], 10, 64)
		order := strings.Compare(leftIDs[i], rightIDs[i])
		switch {
		case xErr == nil && yErr == nil:
			order = int(x - y)
		case xErr == nil:
			order = -1
		case yErr == nil:
			order = 1
		}
		if order != 0 {
			if order < 0 {
				return -1
			}
			return 1
		}
	}
	if len(leftIDs) < len(rightIDs) {
		return -1
	}
	return 1
}

// ---- Testing: messages ----

// These show the messages they compared with inspect, which is linked only
// into programs that use them.

// receiveWait is how long a receive assertion waits when the test gives no
// timeout: 100 ms, or what --receive-timeout set.
func receiveWait(ms int64) int64 {
	if ms >= 0 {
		return ms
	}
	return testSetting("ALCHEM_TEST_RECEIVE_TIMEOUT", 100)
}

// receiveDetails describes a pattern for a failure: as written, with the
// values of the names it pins.
func receiveDetails(pattern string, pinned L[string]) string {
	text := "\n     pattern: " + pattern
	for _, pin := range fwd(pinned) {
		text += "\n     pinned:  " + pin
	}
	return text
}

// assertReceive takes the first message in the mailbox the pattern accepts,
// waiting for one, and leaves the others in place.
func assertReceive[A any](ctx *actor.Context, self *actor.PID[A], ms int64, match func(*actor.Context, A) bool, pattern string, pinned L[string], custom string, where string) A {
	ms = receiveWait(ms)
	received := actor.ReceiveMatching(ctx, self, ms, match, where)
	if !received.E0 {
		problem := custom
		if problem == "" {
			problem = "no message matching the pattern arrived within " + strconv.FormatInt(ms, 10) + "ms"
			if ms == 0 {
				problem = "no message matching the pattern has arrived"
			}
		}
		// As in ExUnit, a long mailbox is shown by its last ten messages.
		waiting := "empty"
		if pending := actor.Pending(self); len(pending) > 0 {
			label := ""
			if len(pending) > 10 {
				label = "(last 10 of " + strconv.Itoa(len(pending)) + ") "
				pending = pending[len(pending)-10:]
			}
			parts := make([]string, len(pending))
			for i, message := range pending {
				parts[i] = inspectValue(message)
			}
			waiting = label + strings.Join(parts, ", ")
		}
		failAt(ctx, where, problem+receiveDetails(pattern, pinned)+"\n     mailbox: "+waiting)
	}
	return received.E1
}

// refuteReceive fails if a message the pattern accepts is in the mailbox or
// arrives in time. The message is taken out.
func refuteReceive[A any](ctx *actor.Context, self *actor.PID[A], ms int64, match func(*actor.Context, A) bool, pattern string, pinned L[string], custom string, where string) {
	if received := actor.ReceiveMatching(ctx, self, receiveWait(ms), match, where); received.E0 {
		problem := custom
		if problem == "" {
			problem = "a message matching the pattern arrived, but none was expected"
		}
		failAt(ctx, where, problem+receiveDetails(pattern, pinned)+"\n     received: "+inspectValue(received.E1))
	}
}

// ---- URI parsing and Version requirements ----

// uriParse splits an address into its parts. Text that is not an address
// becomes a URI holding only a path, as in Elixir.
func uriParse(text string) TURI {
	parsed, err := uriNewParts(text)
	if err != nil {
		return TURI{noneOf[string](), noneOf[string](), noneOf[string](), noneOf[int64](), text, noneOf[string](), noneOf[string]()}
	}
	return parsed
}

func uriNew(text string) Tresult[TURI, string] {
	parsed, err := uriNewParts(text)
	if err != nil {
		return errOf[TURI]("malformed URI " + strconv.Quote(text))
	}
	return okOf(parsed)
}

func uriNewParts(text string) (TURI, error) {
	u, err := url.Parse(text)
	if err != nil {
		return TURI{}, err
	}
	present := func(value string, has bool) Toption[string] {
		if !has {
			return noneOf[string]()
		}
		return someOf(value)
	}
	port := noneOf[int64]()
	if number, err := strconv.ParseInt(u.Port(), 10, 64); err == nil {
		port = someOf(number)
	} else if u.Host != "" {
		// The usual port of the scheme, when the address names none.
		switch u.Scheme {
		case "http", "ws":
			port = someOf(int64(80))
		case "https", "wss":
			port = someOf(int64(443))
		case "ftp":
			port = someOf(int64(21))
		}
	}
	userinfo := noneOf[string]()
	if u.User != nil {
		userinfo = someOf(u.User.String())
	}
	path := u.EscapedPath()
	if u.Opaque != "" {
		path = u.Opaque
	}
	return TURI{present(u.Scheme, u.Scheme != ""), userinfo, present(u.Hostname(), u.Host != ""), port, path,
		present(u.RawQuery, u.RawQuery != "" || u.ForceQuery), present(u.EscapedFragment(), u.Fragment != "")}, nil
}

// uriString writes a URI back as text, leaving out the usual port of its
// scheme.
// uriMerge resolves a reference against a base, as a browser resolves a
// link.
func uriMerge(base, reference string) TURI {
	b, errB := url.Parse(base)
	r, errR := url.Parse(reference)
	if errB != nil || errR != nil {
		return uriParse(reference)
	}
	return uriParse(b.ResolveReference(r).String())
}

func uriMergeStruct(base TURI, reference string) TURI { return uriMerge(uriString(base), reference) }

// uriAppendPath adds a path, which must start with /, to the URI's path.
func uriAppendPath(ctx *actor.Context, u TURI, path string, where string) TURI {
	if !strings.HasPrefix(path, "/") {
		failAt(ctx, where, "URI.append_path needs a path that starts with /")
	}
	u.F4 = strings.TrimSuffix(u.F4, "/") + path
	return u
}

// uriAppendQuery adds a query to the URI's query, joined with &.
func uriAppendQuery(u TURI, query string) TURI {
	if existing, ok := u.F5.(Toption_c1[string]); ok && existing.F0 != "" {
		u.F5 = someOf(existing.F0 + "&" + query)
	} else {
		u.F5 = someOf(query)
	}
	return u
}

func uriString(u TURI) string {
	text := func(o Toption[string]) (string, bool) {
		some, ok := o.(Toption_c1[string])
		return some.F0, ok
	}
	var out strings.Builder
	scheme, hasScheme := text(u.F0)
	if hasScheme {
		out.WriteString(scheme + ":")
	}
	if host, ok := text(u.F2); ok {
		out.WriteString("//")
		if userinfo, ok := text(u.F1); ok {
			out.WriteString(userinfo + "@")
		}
		if strings.Contains(host, ":") {
			host = "[" + host + "]"
		}
		out.WriteString(host)
		if port, ok := u.F3.(Toption_c1[int64]); ok {
			usual := map[string]int64{"http": 80, "ws": 80, "https": 443, "wss": 443, "ftp": 21}[scheme]
			if port.F0 != usual {
				out.WriteString(":" + strconv.FormatInt(port.F0, 10))
			}
		}
	}
	out.WriteString(u.F4)
	if query, ok := text(u.F5); ok {
		out.WriteString("?" + query)
	}
	if fragment, ok := text(u.F6); ok {
		out.WriteString("#" + fragment)
	}
	return out.String()
}

// versionMatch reports whether a version meets a requirement: comparisons
// such as ">= 1.2.0" and "~> 1.4", joined by "and" and "or", where "or"
// binds more loosely.
func versionMatch(ctx *actor.Context, version, requirement string, where string) bool {
	bad := func() { failAt(ctx, where, "invalid requirement "+strconv.Quote(requirement)) }
	words := strings.Fields(requirement)
	if len(words) == 0 {
		bad()
	}
	any, all := false, true
	for i := 0; i < len(words); {
		if i+1 >= len(words) {
			bad()
		}
		operator, target := words[i], words[i+1]
		i += 2
		met := false
		if operator == "~>" {
			// ~> 2.1 allows 2.x from 2.1 up; ~> 2.1.3 allows 2.1.x from 2.1.3 up.
			parts := strings.Split(strings.SplitN(target, "-", 2)[0], ".")
			lower, upper := target, ""
			switch len(parts) {
			case 2:
				major, err := strconv.ParseInt(parts[0], 10, 64)
				if err != nil {
					bad()
				}
				lower, upper = target+".0", strconv.FormatInt(major+1, 10)+".0.0"
			case 3:
				minor, err := strconv.ParseInt(parts[1], 10, 64)
				if err != nil {
					bad()
				}
				upper = parts[0] + "." + strconv.FormatInt(minor+1, 10) + ".0"
			default:
				bad()
			}
			if _, _, ok := versionParts(lower); !ok {
				bad()
			}
			met = versionCompare(ctx, version, lower, where) >= 0 && versionCompare(ctx, version, upper, where) < 0
		} else {
			if _, _, ok := versionParts(target); !ok {
				bad()
			}
			order := versionCompare(ctx, version, target, where)
			switch operator {
			case "==":
				met = order == 0
			case "!=":
				met = order != 0
			case ">":
				met = order > 0
			case ">=":
				met = order >= 0
			case "<":
				met = order < 0
			case "<=":
				met = order <= 0
			default:
				bad()
			}
		}
		all = all && met
		if i == len(words) {
			break
		}
		switch words[i] {
		case "and":
		case "or":
			any, all = any || all, true
		default:
			bad()
		}
		i++
		if i == len(words) {
			bad()
		}
	}
	return any || all
}

// ---- Logger ----

// logLevels orders the levels; a message below the current one is dropped.
var logLevels = []string{"debug", "info", "warning", "error", "none"}

var logCurrent atomic.Int64

func init() {
	if at := slices.Index(logLevels, os.Getenv("ALCHEM_LOG_LEVEL")); at >= 0 {
		logCurrent.Store(int64(at))
	}
}

// The logger's settings: the format of a line, which metadata it shows,
// and the handlers each message goes to besides the console.
var logState = struct {
	sync.Mutex
	format   string
	shown    []string
	handlers map[actor.Atom]func(actor.Atom, string, L[pair[actor.Atom, string]])
	console  bool
	// metadata is each process's own, from Logger.metadata.
	metadata map[*actor.Context][]pair[actor.Atom, string]
}{format: "$time $metadata[$level] $message", console: true, handlers: map[actor.Atom]func(actor.Atom, string, L[pair[actor.Atom, string]]){}, metadata: map[*actor.Context][]pair[actor.Atom, string]{}}

// logWrite sends a message at a level, with the process's metadata and the
// metadata given with it, to the console and the handlers.
func logWrite(level int64, text string) { logWriteWith(nil, level, text, nil) }

func logWriteWith(ctx *actor.Context, level int64, text string, extra []pair[actor.Atom, string]) {
	if level < logCurrent.Load() {
		return
	}
	logState.Lock()
	metadata := append(append([]pair[actor.Atom, string]{}, logState.metadata[ctx]...), extra...)
	format, shown, console := logState.format, logState.shown, logState.console
	handlers := make([]func(actor.Atom, string, L[pair[actor.Atom, string]]), 0, len(logState.handlers))
	for _, handler := range logState.handlers {
		handlers = append(handlers, handler)
	}
	logState.Unlock()
	name := actor.Atom(logLevels[level])
	for _, handler := range handlers {
		handler(name, text, mk(slices.Clone(metadata)))
	}
	if !console {
		return
	}
	var meta []string
	for _, m := range metadata {
		if slices.Contains(shown, "all") || slices.Contains(shown, string(m.E0)) {
			meta = append(meta, string(m.E0)+"="+m.E1)
		}
	}
	metaText := strings.Join(meta, " ")
	if metaText != "" {
		metaText += " "
	}
	line := strings.NewReplacer("$time", time.Now().Format("15:04:05.000"), "$date", time.Now().Format("2006-01-02"), "$level", string(name), "$metadata", metaText, "$message", text, "$node", "").Replace(format)
	os.Stderr.WriteString(strings.TrimRight(line, "\n") + "\n")
}

func logDebug(text string)   { logWrite(0, text) }
func logInfo(text string)    { logWrite(1, text) }
func logWarning(text string) { logWrite(2, text) }
func logError(text string)   { logWrite(3, text) }

func logLevelIndex(ctx *actor.Context, level actor.Atom, where string) int64 {
	at := slices.Index(logLevels, string(level))
	if at < 0 || level == "none" {
		failAt(ctx, where, "the log level is :debug, :info, :warning or :error, not :"+string(level))
	}
	return int64(at)
}

// The forms of the level functions: with metadata, and with a function that
// makes the message only when the level is enabled.
func logAt(level int64) func(*actor.Context, string, L[pair[actor.Atom, string]]) {
	return func(ctx *actor.Context, text string, metadata L[pair[actor.Atom, string]]) {
		logWriteWith(ctx, level, text, fwd(metadata))
	}
}

func logLazy(level int64) func(*actor.Context, func(*actor.Context) string) {
	return func(ctx *actor.Context, message func(*actor.Context) string) {
		if level >= logCurrent.Load() {
			logWriteWith(ctx, level, message(ctx), nil)
		}
	}
}

func logLog(ctx *actor.Context, level actor.Atom, text string, where string) {
	logWriteWith(ctx, logLevelIndex(ctx, level, where), text, nil)
}

// logMetadata adds to the calling process's metadata; a key given again
// replaces its value.
func logMetadata(ctx *actor.Context, metadata L[pair[actor.Atom, string]]) {
	logState.Lock()
	defer logState.Unlock()
	_, known := logState.metadata[ctx]
	current := logState.metadata[ctx]
	for _, entry := range fwd(metadata) {
		current = slices.DeleteFunc(current, func(p pair[actor.Atom, string]) bool { return p.E0 == entry.E0 })
		current = append(current, entry)
	}
	logState.metadata[ctx] = current
	if !known {
		actor.OnEnd(ctx, func() {
			logState.Lock()
			delete(logState.metadata, ctx)
			logState.Unlock()
		})
	}
}

func logCurrentMetadata(ctx *actor.Context) L[pair[actor.Atom, string]] {
	logState.Lock()
	defer logState.Unlock()
	return mk(slices.Clone(logState.metadata[ctx]))
}

// logConfigureOptions sets level:, format: (with $time, $date, $level,
// $metadata and $message) and metadata: (the keys to show, separated by
// commas, or "all").
func logConfigureOptions(ctx *actor.Context, options L[pair[actor.Atom, string]], where string) {
	for _, option := range fwd(options) {
		switch option.E0 {
		case "level":
			logConfigure(ctx, option.E1, where)
		case "format":
			logState.Lock()
			logState.format = option.E1
			logState.Unlock()
		case "metadata":
			logState.Lock()
			logState.shown = strings.Split(option.E1, ",")
			for i := range logState.shown {
				logState.shown[i] = strings.TrimSpace(logState.shown[i])
			}
			logState.Unlock()
		default:
			failAt(ctx, where, "Logger.configure takes level:, format: and metadata:")
		}
	}
}

// logAddHandler sends every message that passes the level to the function,
// in the process that logs it; :console names the console itself.
func logAddHandler[B any](ctx *actor.Context, name actor.Atom, handle func(*actor.Context, actor.Atom, string, L[pair[actor.Atom, string]]) B) bool {
	logState.Lock()
	defer logState.Unlock()
	if name == "console" {
		was := logState.console
		logState.console = true
		return !was
	}
	if _, taken := logState.handlers[name]; taken {
		return false
	}
	logState.handlers[name] = func(level actor.Atom, text string, metadata L[pair[actor.Atom, string]]) {
		handle(ctx, level, text, metadata)
	}
	return true
}

func logRemoveHandler(name actor.Atom) bool {
	logState.Lock()
	defer logState.Unlock()
	if name == "console" {
		was := logState.console
		logState.console = false
		return was
	}
	_, had := logState.handlers[name]
	delete(logState.handlers, name)
	return had
}

func logConfigure(ctx *actor.Context, level string, where string) {
	at := slices.Index(logLevels, level)
	if at < 0 {
		failAt(ctx, where, "the log level is debug, info, warning, error or none, not "+strconv.Quote(level))
	}
	logCurrent.Store(int64(at))
}

func logLevel() string { return logLevels[logCurrent.Load()] }

// ---- OptionParser ----

type optionResult = struct {
	E0 actor.Map[string, string]
	E1 L[string]
	E2 L[pair[string, string]]
}

func optionParse(ctx *actor.Context, arguments iter.Seq[string], switches actor.Map[string, string], where string) optionResult {
	return optionParseAliases(ctx, arguments, switches, actor.Map[string, string]{}, where)
}

// optionParseAliases reads switches from command-line arguments. switches
// maps a name to its type: boolean, string, integer, float or count.
// aliases maps a short letter to a name. It returns the values as text by
// name, the arguments that are not switches, and the switches that are
// unknown or have a value of the wrong type.
func optionParseAliases(ctx *actor.Context, arguments iter.Seq[string], switches, aliases actor.Map[string, string], where string) optionResult {
	actor.MapEach(switches, func(name, kind string) {
		if !slices.Contains([]string{"boolean", "string", "integer", "float", "count"}, kind) {
			failAt(ctx, where, "the type of switch "+strconv.Quote(name)+" is boolean, string, integer, float or count, not "+strconv.Quote(kind))
		}
	})
	values := actor.NewMapBuilder(actor.Map[string, string]{})
	var rest []string
	var invalid []pair[string, string]
	all := collect(arguments)
	for i := 0; i < len(all); i++ {
		argument := all[i]
		if argument == "--" {
			rest = append(rest, all[i+1:]...)
			break
		}
		if !strings.HasPrefix(argument, "-") || argument == "-" {
			rest = append(rest, argument)
			continue
		}
		written, attached, hasValue := strings.Cut(argument, "=")
		name := strings.ReplaceAll(strings.TrimLeft(written, "-"), "-", "_")
		if !strings.HasPrefix(written, "--") {
			long, known := actor.MapGet(aliases, name)
			if !known {
				invalid = append(invalid, pair[string, string]{written, attached})
				continue
			}
			name = long
		}
		kind, known := actor.MapGet(switches, name)
		negated := false
		if !known && strings.HasPrefix(name, "no_") {
			if positive, ok := actor.MapGet(switches, name[3:]); ok && positive == "boolean" {
				name, kind, known, negated = name[3:], positive, true, true
			}
		}
		if !known {
			invalid = append(invalid, pair[string, string]{written, attached})
			continue
		}
		switch kind {
		case "boolean":
			value := "true"
			if negated {
				value = "false"
			}
			if hasValue {
				if attached != "true" && attached != "false" {
					invalid = append(invalid, pair[string, string]{written, attached})
					continue
				}
				value = attached
			}
			values.Put(name, value)
			continue
		case "count":
			seen, _ := values.Get(name)
			n, _ := strconv.Atoi(seen)
			values.Put(name, strconv.Itoa(n+1))
			continue
		}
		// The value follows, attached with = or as the next argument.
		if !hasValue {
			if i+1 >= len(all) {
				invalid = append(invalid, pair[string, string]{written, ""})
				continue
			}
			i++
			attached = all[i]
		}
		ok := true
		switch kind {
		case "integer":
			_, err := strconv.ParseInt(attached, 10, 64)
			ok = err == nil
		case "float":
			_, err := strconv.ParseFloat(attached, 64)
			ok = err == nil
		}
		if !ok {
			invalid = append(invalid, pair[string, string]{written, attached})
			continue
		}
		values.Put(name, attached)
	}
	return optionResult{values.Map(), mk(rest), mk(invalid)}
}

// optionsResult is what the keyword form of OptionParser.parse returns: the
// switches as a keyword list of their values as text, in order, the other
// arguments, and the invalid switches with their values when they had one.
type optionsResult = struct {
	E0 L[pair[actor.Atom, string]]
	E1 L[string]
	E2 L[pair[string, Toption[string]]]
}

// optionParseKeyword is OptionParser.parse with Elixir's options: strict: or
// switches: (a keyword list of name: type, the types being :boolean,
// :string, :integer, :float, :count and :keep) and aliases: (letter: name).
// Under strict: an unknown switch is invalid; under switches: it is kept,
// as a string, or as a boolean when no value follows.
func optionParseKeyword(ctx *actor.Context, arguments iter.Seq[string], options L[pair[actor.Atom, L[pair[actor.Atom, actor.Atom]]]], where string) optionsResult {
	return optionParseWith(ctx, collect(arguments), options, false, where)
}

func optionParseHead(ctx *actor.Context, arguments iter.Seq[string], options L[pair[actor.Atom, L[pair[actor.Atom, actor.Atom]]]], where string) optionsResult {
	return optionParseWith(ctx, collect(arguments), options, true, where)
}

func optionParseWith(ctx *actor.Context, all []string, options L[pair[actor.Atom, L[pair[actor.Atom, actor.Atom]]]], head bool, where string) optionsResult {
	types, aliases := map[string]string{}, map[string]string{}
	strict, given := false, false
	for _, option := range fwd(options) {
		switch option.E0 {
		case "strict", "switches":
			if given {
				failAt(ctx, where, "OptionParser.parse takes strict: or switches:, not both")
			}
			strict, given = option.E0 == "strict", true
			for _, entry := range fwd(option.E1) {
				kind := string(entry.E1)
				if !slices.Contains([]string{"boolean", "string", "integer", "float", "count", "keep"}, kind) {
					failAt(ctx, where, "the type of :"+string(entry.E0)+" is :boolean, :string, :integer, :float, :count or :keep")
				}
				types[string(entry.E0)] = kind
			}
		case "aliases":
			for _, entry := range fwd(option.E1) {
				aliases[string(entry.E0)] = string(entry.E1)
			}
		default:
			failAt(ctx, where, "OptionParser.parse takes strict:, switches: and aliases:")
		}
	}
	var parsed []pair[actor.Atom, string]
	var rest []string
	var invalid []pair[string, Toption[string]]
	set := func(name, value string) {
		if types[name] != "keep" {
			parsed = slices.DeleteFunc(parsed, func(p pair[actor.Atom, string]) bool { return string(p.E0) == name })
		}
		parsed = append(parsed, pair[actor.Atom, string]{actor.Atom(name), value})
	}
	bad := func(written string, value string, has bool) {
		v := noneOf[string]()
		if has {
			v = someOf(value)
		}
		invalid = append(invalid, pair[string, Toption[string]]{written, v})
	}
	for i := 0; i < len(all); i++ {
		argument := all[i]
		if argument == "--" {
			rest = append(rest, all[i+1:]...)
			break
		}
		if !strings.HasPrefix(argument, "-") || argument == "-" {
			if head {
				rest = append(rest, all[i:]...)
				break
			}
			rest = append(rest, argument)
			continue
		}
		written, attached, hasValue := strings.Cut(argument, "=")
		name := strings.ReplaceAll(strings.TrimLeft(written, "-"), "-", "_")
		if !strings.HasPrefix(written, "--") {
			long, known := aliases[name]
			if !known {
				bad(written, attached, hasValue)
				continue
			}
			name = long
		}
		kind, known := types[name]
		negated := false
		if !known && strings.HasPrefix(name, "no_") && types[name[3:]] == "boolean" {
			name, kind, known, negated = name[3:], "boolean", true, true
		}
		if !known {
			if strict {
				bad(written, attached, hasValue)
				continue
			}
			// Without strict, an unknown switch takes the next argument as
			// its value when it is not a switch, and is true otherwise.
			kind = "boolean"
			if hasValue || i+1 < len(all) && !strings.HasPrefix(all[i+1], "-") {
				kind = "string"
			}
		}
		switch kind {
		case "boolean":
			value := strconv.FormatBool(!negated)
			if hasValue {
				if attached != "true" && attached != "false" {
					bad(written, attached, true)
					continue
				}
				value = attached
			}
			set(name, value)
			continue
		case "count":
			n := 0
			for _, p := range parsed {
				if string(p.E0) == name {
					n, _ = strconv.Atoi(p.E1)
				}
			}
			set(name, strconv.Itoa(n+1))
			continue
		}
		if !hasValue {
			if i+1 >= len(all) {
				bad(written, "", false)
				continue
			}
			i++
			attached = all[i]
		}
		ok := true
		switch kind {
		case "integer":
			_, err := strconv.ParseInt(attached, 10, 64)
			ok = err == nil
		case "float":
			_, err := strconv.ParseFloat(attached, 64)
			ok = err == nil
		}
		if !ok {
			bad(written, attached, true)
			continue
		}
		set(name, attached)
	}
	return optionsResult{mk(parsed), mk(rest), mk(invalid)}
}

// optionToArgv writes switches back as command-line arguments: name: value
// as --name value, and a boolean as --name or --no-name.
func optionToArgv(options L[pair[actor.Atom, string]]) L[string] {
	var out []string
	for _, option := range fwd(options) {
		name := "--" + strings.ReplaceAll(string(option.E0), "_", "-")
		switch option.E1 {
		case "true":
			out = append(out, name)
		case "false":
			out = append(out, "--no-"+strings.ReplaceAll(string(option.E0), "_", "-"))
		default:
			out = append(out, name, option.E1)
		}
	}
	return mk(out)
}

// optionSplit splits a command line into arguments as a shell does: at
// spaces outside quotes, with backslash escapes.
func optionSplit(line string) L[string] {
	var out []string
	var current strings.Builder
	quote, started := rune(0), false
	for i := 0; i < len(line); i++ {
		ch := rune(line[i])
		switch {
		case ch == '\\' && i+1 < len(line) && quote != '\'':
			i++
			current.WriteByte(line[i])
			started = true
		case quote != 0 && ch == quote:
			quote = 0
		case quote == 0 && (ch == '"' || ch == '\''):
			quote, started = ch, true
		case quote == 0 && (ch == ' ' || ch == '\t' || ch == '\n'):
			if started {
				out = append(out, current.String())
				current.Reset()
				started = false
			}
		default:
			current.WriteByte(line[i])
			started = true
		}
	}
	if started {
		out = append(out, current.String())
	}
	return mk(out)
}

// ---- Calendar: time zones ----

// The functions that put a DateTime in a zone bring the zone database with
// them, so that a program behaves the same where none is installed.
func init() {
	calLoadZone = func(name string) (*time.Location, bool) {
		zone, err := time.LoadLocation(name)
		return zone, err == nil
	}
}

func calShiftZone(d TDateTime, name string) Tresult[TDateTime, string] {
	moved, ok := calIn(calFromDateTime(d), name)
	if !ok {
		return errOf[TDateTime]("unknown time zone " + strconv.Quote(name))
	}
	return okOf(moved)
}

func calShiftZoneBang(ctx *actor.Context, d TDateTime, name string, where string) TDateTime {
	moved, ok := calIn(calFromDateTime(d), name)
	if !ok {
		failAt(ctx, where, "unknown time zone "+strconv.Quote(name))
	}
	return moved
}

func calNowIn(name string) Tresult[TDateTime, string] {
	return calShiftZone(calDateTimeOf(time.Now()), name)
}

// calFromNaiveIn reads a date and time as the wall clock of a zone. A time
// the clocks skipped moves forward by the gap, and one they repeated takes
// its first occurrence.
func calFromNaiveIn(n TNaiveDateTime, name string) Tresult[TDateTime, string] {
	zone, ok := calZone(name)
	if !ok {
		return errOf[TDateTime]("unknown time zone " + strconv.Quote(name))
	}
	if !calValid(n.F0, n.F1, n.F2, n.F3, n.F4, n.F5, n.F6) {
		return errOf[TDateTime]("invalid date or time")
	}
	moment := time.Date(int(n.F0), time.Month(n.F1), int(n.F2), int(n.F3), int(n.F4), int(n.F5), int(n.F6)*1000, zone)
	moved, _ := calIn(moment, name)
	return okOf(moved)
}

// ---- Atom and Keyword ----

func atomToString(a actor.Atom) string   { return string(a) }
func atomFromString(s string) actor.Atom { return actor.Atom(s) }

// A keyword list is a list of {atom, value} pairs. The first pair with a
// key is the one lookups see; a key may appear more than once.
type keyword[V any] = L[pair[actor.Atom, V]]

func keywordGet[V any](list keyword[V], key actor.Atom) Toption[V] {
	for i := len(list.S) - 1; i >= 0; i-- {
		if list.S[i].E0 == key {
			return someOf(list.S[i].E1)
		}
	}
	return noneOf[V]()
}

func keywordGetOr[V any](list keyword[V], key actor.Atom, fallback V) V {
	if found, ok := keywordGet(list, key).(Toption_c1[V]); ok {
		return found.F0
	}
	return fallback
}

func keywordFetchBang[V any](ctx *actor.Context, list keyword[V], key actor.Atom, where string) V {
	found, ok := keywordGet(list, key).(Toption_c1[V])
	if !ok {
		failAt(ctx, where, "key :"+string(key)+" not found")
	}
	return found.F0
}

func keywordGetValues[V any](list keyword[V], key actor.Atom) L[V] {
	var out []V
	for _, entry := range fwd(list) {
		if entry.E0 == key {
			out = append(out, entry.E1)
		}
	}
	return mk(out)
}

func keywordHasKey[V any](list keyword[V], key actor.Atom) bool {
	_, ok := keywordGet(list, key).(Toption_c1[V])
	return ok
}

// keywordWithout keeps the pairs whose key the function does not reject.
func keywordWithout[V any](list keyword[V], reject func(actor.Atom) bool) []pair[actor.Atom, V] {
	var out []pair[actor.Atom, V]
	for _, entry := range fwd(list) {
		if !reject(entry.E0) {
			out = append(out, entry)
		}
	}
	return out
}

func keywordDelete[V any](list keyword[V], key actor.Atom) keyword[V] {
	return mk(keywordWithout(list, func(k actor.Atom) bool { return k == key }))
}

// put removes every pair with the key and adds the new one at the front.
func keywordPut[V any](list keyword[V], key actor.Atom, value V) keyword[V] {
	rest := keywordWithout(list, func(k actor.Atom) bool { return k == key })
	return mk(append([]pair[actor.Atom, V]{{key, value}}, rest...))
}

func keywordPutNew[V any](list keyword[V], key actor.Atom, value V) keyword[V] {
	if keywordHasKey(list, key) {
		return list
	}
	return keywordPut(list, key, value)
}

func keywordKeys[V any](list keyword[V]) L[actor.Atom] {
	out := make([]actor.Atom, 0, len(list.S))
	for _, entry := range fwd(list) {
		out = append(out, entry.E0)
	}
	return mk(out)
}

func keywordValues[V any](list keyword[V]) L[V] {
	out := make([]V, 0, len(list.S))
	for _, entry := range fwd(list) {
		out = append(out, entry.E1)
	}
	return mk(out)
}

// merge keeps the first list's pairs whose keys the second lacks, followed
// by the second list.
func keywordMerge[V any](a, b keyword[V]) keyword[V] {
	return mk(append(keywordWithout(a, func(k actor.Atom) bool { return keywordHasKey(b, k) }), fwd(b)...))
}

func keywordTake[V any](list keyword[V], keys L[actor.Atom]) keyword[V] {
	return mk(keywordWithout(list, func(k actor.Atom) bool { return !slices.Contains(keys.S, k) }))
}

func keywordDrop[V any](list keyword[V], keys L[actor.Atom]) keyword[V] {
	return mk(keywordWithout(list, func(k actor.Atom) bool { return slices.Contains(keys.S, k) }))
}

func keywordUpdate[V any](ctx *actor.Context, list keyword[V], key actor.Atom, initial V, f func(*actor.Context, V) V) keyword[V] {
	if found, ok := keywordGet(list, key).(Toption_c1[V]); ok {
		out := fwd(list)
		for i := range out {
			if out[i].E0 == key {
				out[i].E1 = f(ctx, found.F0)
				break
			}
		}
		return mk(out)
	}
	return mk(append(fwd(list), pair[actor.Atom, V]{key, initial}))
}

func keywordGetLazy[V any](ctx *actor.Context, list keyword[V], key actor.Atom, f func(*actor.Context) V) V {
	if value, ok := keywordGet(list, key).(Toption_c1[V]); ok {
		return value.F0
	}
	return f(ctx)
}

func keywordPutNewLazy[V any](ctx *actor.Context, list keyword[V], key actor.Atom, f func(*actor.Context) V) keyword[V] {
	if keywordHasKey(list, key) {
		return list
	}
	return keywordPut(list, key, f(ctx))
}

func keywordPopLazy[V any](ctx *actor.Context, list keyword[V], key actor.Atom, f func(*actor.Context) V) pair[V, keyword[V]] {
	return pair[V, keyword[V]]{keywordGetLazy(ctx, list, key, f), keywordDelete(list, key)}
}

// keywordMergeWith resolves a key in both lists with the function, given
// the key and both values.
func keywordMergeWith[V any](ctx *actor.Context, a, b keyword[V], resolve func(*actor.Context, actor.Atom, V, V) V) keyword[V] {
	out := fwd(a)
	for _, entry := range fwd(b) {
		found := false
		for i := range out {
			if out[i].E0 == entry.E0 {
				out[i].E1, found = resolve(ctx, entry.E0, out[i].E1, entry.E1), true
			}
		}
		if !found {
			out = append(out, entry)
		}
	}
	return mk(out)
}

// keywordSplit separates the pairs with the given keys from the others.
func keywordSplit[V any](list keyword[V], keys L[actor.Atom]) pair[keyword[V], keyword[V]] {
	wanted := func(k actor.Atom) bool { return slices.Contains(keys.S, k) }
	return pair[keyword[V], keyword[V]]{mk(keywordWithout(list, func(k actor.Atom) bool { return !wanted(k) })), mk(keywordWithout(list, wanted))}
}

// keywordValidate checks that the list has only the keys given, filling in
// the defaults of those missing: ok(list), or error(the unknown keys).
func keywordValidate[V any](list keyword[V], allowed keyword[V]) Tresult[keyword[V], L[actor.Atom]] {
	var unknown []actor.Atom
	for _, entry := range fwd(list) {
		if !keywordHasKey(allowed, entry.E0) && !slices.Contains(unknown, entry.E0) {
			unknown = append(unknown, entry.E0)
		}
	}
	if len(unknown) > 0 {
		return Tresult_c1[keyword[V], L[actor.Atom]]{mk(unknown)}
	}
	out := fwd(list)
	for _, entry := range fwd(allowed) {
		if !keywordHasKey(list, entry.E0) {
			out = append(out, entry)
		}
	}
	return Tresult_c0[keyword[V], L[actor.Atom]]{mk(out)}
}

func keywordValidateKeys[V any](list keyword[V], allowed L[actor.Atom]) Tresult[keyword[V], L[actor.Atom]] {
	var unknown []actor.Atom
	for _, entry := range fwd(list) {
		if !slices.Contains(allowed.S, entry.E0) && !slices.Contains(unknown, entry.E0) {
			unknown = append(unknown, entry.E0)
		}
	}
	if len(unknown) > 0 {
		return Tresult_c1[keyword[V], L[actor.Atom]]{mk(unknown)}
	}
	return Tresult_c0[keyword[V], L[actor.Atom]]{list}
}

func keywordPop[V any](list keyword[V], key actor.Atom) pair[Toption[V], keyword[V]] {
	return pair[Toption[V], keyword[V]]{keywordGet(list, key), keywordDelete(list, key)}
}

// to_map keeps the first value of each key.
func keywordToMap[V any](list keyword[V]) actor.Map[actor.Atom, V] {
	out := actor.NewMapBuilder(actor.Map[actor.Atom, V]{})
	for i := 0; i < len(list.S); i++ {
		out.Put(list.S[i].E0, list.S[i].E1)
	}
	return out.Map()
}

// ---- Port ----

// A port is an external program with its standard input held open. A
// process of its own reads what the program writes and sends it, a line at
// a time, to the port's owner, followed by the exit status.
type portHandle struct {
	command *exec.Cmd
	input   io.WriteCloser
	lock    sync.Mutex
	closed  bool
	// name is the program; header is the size of a packet's length header,
	// or 0; written and read count the bytes each way.
	name          string
	header        int
	written, read atomic.Int64
	exited        bool
	watchers      []*actor.PID[TPortEvent]
}

func portOpen(ctx *actor.Context, program string, arguments iter.Seq[string], owner *actor.PID[TPortEvent], where string) Tresult[*portHandle, string] {
	return portOpenWith(ctx, program, arguments, owner, L[pair[actor.Atom, actor.Atom]]{}, where)
}

// portOpenPacket frames what goes each way with a length header of 1, 2 or
// 4 bytes, as packet: N does in Elixir.
func portOpenPacket(ctx *actor.Context, program string, arguments iter.Seq[string], owner *actor.PID[TPortEvent], options L[pair[actor.Atom, int64]], where string) Tresult[*portHandle, string] {
	header := 0
	for _, option := range fwd(options) {
		if option.E0 != "packet" || (option.E1 != 1 && option.E1 != 2 && option.E1 != 4) {
			failAt(ctx, where, "Port.open takes packet: 1, 2 or 4")
		}
		header = int(option.E1)
	}
	return portStart(ctx, program, arguments, owner, "packet", header, "merge", where)
}

// portOpenWith takes mode: :line (each line an event, the default) or
// :binary (output as it comes), and stderr: :merge (the default),
// :discard or :inherit (the program's own standard error).
func portOpenWith(ctx *actor.Context, program string, arguments iter.Seq[string], owner *actor.PID[TPortEvent], options L[pair[actor.Atom, actor.Atom]], where string) Tresult[*portHandle, string] {
	mode, stderr := "line", "merge"
	for _, option := range fwd(options) {
		switch {
		case option.E0 == "mode" && (option.E1 == "line" || option.E1 == "binary"):
			mode = string(option.E1)
		case option.E0 == "stderr" && (option.E1 == "merge" || option.E1 == "discard" || option.E1 == "inherit"):
			stderr = string(option.E1)
		default:
			failAt(ctx, where, "Port.open takes mode: :line or :binary and stderr: :merge, :discard or :inherit")
		}
	}
	return portStart(ctx, program, arguments, owner, mode, 0, stderr, where)
}

func portStart(ctx *actor.Context, program string, arguments iter.Seq[string], owner *actor.PID[TPortEvent], mode string, header int, stderr string, where string) Tresult[*portHandle, string] {
	command := exec.Command(program, collect(arguments)...)
	input, err := command.StdinPipe()
	if err != nil {
		return errOf[*portHandle](err.Error())
	}
	output, err := command.StdoutPipe()
	if err != nil {
		return errOf[*portHandle](err.Error())
	}
	switch stderr {
	case "merge":
		command.Stderr = command.Stdout
	case "inherit":
		command.Stderr = os.Stderr
	}
	if err := command.Start(); err != nil {
		return errOf[*portHandle](err.Error())
	}
	port := &portHandle{command: command, input: input, name: program, header: header}
	actor.Spawn(ctx, func(reader *actor.Context, _ *actor.PID[struct{}]) int64 {
		// A stop request ends the program, which ends the reading.
		finished := make(chan struct{})
		defer close(finished)
		go func() {
			select {
			case <-actor.Stopping(reader):
				command.Process.Kill()
			case <-finished:
			}
		}()
		send := func(text string) {
			port.read.Add(int64(len(text)))
			actor.Send(reader, owner, TPortEvent(TPortEvent_c0{text}), -1, where)
		}
		data := bufio.NewReader(output)
		switch mode {
		case "line":
			for {
				line, err := data.ReadString('\n')
				if line != "" {
					send(strings.TrimRight(line, "\r\n"))
				}
				if err != nil {
					break
				}
			}
		case "binary":
			buffer := make([]byte, 64<<10)
			for {
				n, err := data.Read(buffer)
				if n > 0 {
					send(string(buffer[:n]))
				}
				if err != nil {
					break
				}
			}
		case "packet":
			for {
				head := make([]byte, header)
				if _, err := io.ReadFull(data, head); err != nil {
					break
				}
				size := 0
				for _, b := range head {
					size = size<<8 | int(b)
				}
				body := make([]byte, size)
				if _, err := io.ReadFull(data, body); err != nil {
					break
				}
				send(string(body))
			}
		}
		status := int64(0)
		if err := command.Wait(); err != nil {
			status = -1
			var exit *exec.ExitError
			if errors.As(err, &exit) {
				status = int64(exit.ExitCode())
			}
		}
		port.lock.Lock()
		port.exited = true
		watchers := port.watchers
		port.lock.Unlock()
		actor.Send(reader, owner, TPortEvent(TPortEvent_c1{status}), -1, where)
		for _, watcher := range watchers {
			actor.Deliver(watcher, TPortEvent(TPortEvent_c1{status}), time.Second)
		}
		return 0
	}, 1, where)
	return okOf(port)
}

// portInfo describes a port whose program is still running.
func portInfo(port *portHandle) Toption[TPort_oInfo] {
	port.lock.Lock()
	defer port.lock.Unlock()
	if port.exited {
		return noneOf[TPort_oInfo]()
	}
	return someOf(TPort_oInfo{port.name, int64(port.command.Process.Pid), port.written.Load(), port.read.Load()})
}

// portMonitor sends the program's exit status to another process too, as
// PortEvent.exit, when it ends; false when it has already ended.
func portMonitor(port *portHandle, watcher *actor.PID[TPortEvent]) bool {
	port.lock.Lock()
	defer port.lock.Unlock()
	if port.exited {
		return false
	}
	port.watchers = append(port.watchers, watcher)
	return true
}

// portCommand writes text to the program's standard input.
func portCommand(port *portHandle, text string) bool {
	port.lock.Lock()
	defer port.lock.Unlock()
	if port.closed {
		return false
	}
	if port.header > 0 {
		if port.header < 4 && len(text) >= 1<<(8*port.header) {
			return false
		}
		prefix := make([]byte, port.header)
		for i := range prefix {
			prefix[port.header-1-i] = byte(len(text) >> (8 * i))
		}
		text = string(prefix) + text
	}
	n, err := io.WriteString(port.input, text)
	port.written.Add(int64(n))
	return err == nil
}

// portClose closes the program's standard input, which lets a program that
// reads to the end finish; false when it was already closed.
func portClose(port *portHandle) bool {
	port.lock.Lock()
	defer port.lock.Unlock()
	if port.closed {
		return false
	}
	port.closed = true
	port.input.Close()
	return true
}

func portPid(port *portHandle) int64 { return int64(port.command.Process.Pid) }

// ---- Node ----

func nodeStart(ctx *actor.Context, name, address, cookie string) Tresult[struct{}, string] {
	return nodeStartWith(ctx, name, address, cookie, true)
}

func nodeStartWith(ctx *actor.Context, name, address, cookie string, encrypted bool) Tresult[struct{}, string] {
	if problem := node.Start(ctx, name, address, cookie, encrypted); problem != "" {
		return errOf[struct{}](problem)
	}
	return okOf(struct{}{})
}

// The options are a keyword list; encrypted: false turns encryption off.
func nodeStartOptions(ctx *actor.Context, name, address, cookie string, options L[pair[actor.Atom, bool]]) Tresult[struct{}, string] {
	encrypted := true
	for _, option := range fwd(options) {
		switch option.E0 {
		case "encrypted":
			encrypted = option.E1
		default:
			return errOf[struct{}]("unknown option :" + string(option.E0) + "; Node.start takes encrypted:")
		}
	}
	return nodeStartWith(ctx, name, address, cookie, encrypted)
}

// nodeStartEnv takes the cookie from the ALCHEM_COOKIE environment variable.
func nodeStartEnv(ctx *actor.Context, name, address string) Tresult[struct{}, string] {
	cookie := os.Getenv("ALCHEM_COOKIE")
	if cookie == "" {
		return errOf[struct{}]("set the ALCHEM_COOKIE environment variable, or pass the cookie to Node.start")
	}
	return nodeStart(ctx, name, address, cookie)
}

func nodeSelf(ctx *actor.Context) string    { return node.Name(ctx) }
func nodeAddress(ctx *actor.Context) string { return node.Address(ctx) }

func nodeConnect(ctx *actor.Context, address string) Tresult[string, string] {
	name, problem := node.Connect(ctx, address)
	if problem != "" {
		return errOf[string](problem)
	}
	return okOf(name)
}

// nodeConnectOptions takes reconnect: true to keep the connection, making
// it again whenever it is lost.
func nodeConnectOptions(ctx *actor.Context, address string, options L[pair[actor.Atom, bool]], where string) Tresult[string, string] {
	keep := false
	for _, option := range fwd(options) {
		if option.E0 != "reconnect" {
			failAt(ctx, where, "Node.connect takes reconnect:")
		}
		keep = option.E1
	}
	if !keep {
		return nodeConnect(ctx, address)
	}
	name, problem := node.Reconnect(ctx, address)
	if problem != "" {
		return errOf[string](problem)
	}
	return okOf(name)
}

// The codecs of a call to a function served with Node.serve: the argument
// with the process to reply to, and the reply, ok(value) or error(reason).
type rpcRequest[A, B any] = pair[A, *actor.PID[Tresult[B, string]]]

func rpcCodecs[A, B any](a node.Codec[A], b node.Codec[B]) (node.Codec[rpcRequest[A, B]], node.Codec[Tresult[B, string]]) {
	reply := node.Codec[Tresult[B, string]]{
		Type: "result[" + b.Type + ",string]",
		Encode: func(e *node.Encoder, r Tresult[B, string]) {
			if ok, is := r.(Tresult_c0[B, string]); is {
				e.Bool(true)
				b.Encode(e, ok.F0)
				return
			}
			e.Bool(false)
			e.String(r.(Tresult_c1[B, string]).F0)
		},
		Decode: func(d *node.Decoder) Tresult[B, string] {
			if d.Bool() {
				return okOf(b.Decode(d))
			}
			return errOf[B](d.String())
		},
	}
	request := node.Codec[rpcRequest[A, B]]{
		Type: "tuple[" + a.Type + ",pid[" + reply.Type + "]]",
		Encode: func(e *node.Encoder, r rpcRequest[A, B]) {
			a.Encode(e, r.E0)
			node.EncodePID(e, r.E1, reply)
		},
		Decode: func(d *node.Decoder) rpcRequest[A, B] {
			return rpcRequest[A, B]{a.Decode(d), node.DecodePID(d, reply)}
		},
	}
	return request, reply
}

// nodeServe offers a function to other nodes under a name: each call runs it
// in a process of its own, and a failure becomes error(reason).
func nodeServe[A, B any](ctx *actor.Context, name actor.Atom, f func(*actor.Context, A) B, codecA node.Codec[A], codecB node.Codec[B], where string) bool {
	request, _ := rpcCodecs(codecA, codecB)
	served := actor.Spawn(ctx, func(w *actor.Context, self *actor.PID[rpcRequest[A, B]]) int64 {
		for {
			call := actor.Receive(w, self, -1, where).E1
			actor.Spawn(w, func(worker *actor.Context, _ *actor.PID[struct{}]) int64 {
				task := actor.StartUnlinkedTask(worker, func(c *actor.Context) B { return f(c, call.E0) }, where)
				outcome := actor.YieldTask(worker, task, -1, where)
				answer := okOf(outcome.Value)
				if outcome.Reason != "" {
					answer = errOf[B](outcome.Reason)
				}
				actor.Send(worker, call.E1, answer, 5000, where)
				return 0
			}, 1, where)
		}
	}, 1024, where)
	if !node.Register(ctx, served, "rpc:"+string(name), request) {
		actor.Stop(ctx, served, where)
		return false
	}
	return true
}

// nodeCall runs a function another node serves, waiting up to ms
// milliseconds for its result: ok(value), or error with why there is none.
func nodeCall[A, B any](ctx *actor.Context, other string, name actor.Atom, argument A, ms int64, codecA node.Codec[A], codecB node.Codec[B], where string) Tresult[B, string] {
	request, _ := rpcCodecs(codecA, codecB)
	var target *actor.PID[rpcRequest[A, B]]
	var found bool
	if other == node.Name(ctx) {
		target, found = node.Whereis(ctx, "rpc:"+string(name), request)
	} else {
		target, found = node.RemoteWhereis(ctx, other, "rpc:"+string(name), ms, request)
	}
	if !found {
		return errOf[B]("nothing served as :" + string(name) + " on " + other)
	}
	answer := make(chan Tresult[B, string], 1)
	reply := actor.Spawn(ctx, func(w *actor.Context, self *actor.PID[Tresult[B, string]]) int64 {
		if got := actor.Receive(w, self, ms, where); got.E0 {
			answer <- got.E1
		}
		return 0
	}, 1, where)
	if !actor.Send(ctx, target, rpcRequest[A, B]{argument, reply}, ms, where) {
		actor.Stop(ctx, reply, where)
		return errOf[B]("the call could not be sent")
	}
	timer, stop := actor.After(max(ms, 1))
	defer stop()
	select {
	case result := <-answer:
		return result
	case <-timer:
		actor.Stop(ctx, reply, where)
		return errOf[B]("timeout")
	case <-actor.Stopping(ctx):
		actor.Check(ctx)
	}
	return errOf[B]("timeout")
}

func nodeCallDefault[A, B any](ctx *actor.Context, other string, name actor.Atom, argument A, codecA node.Codec[A], codecB node.Codec[B], where string) Tresult[B, string] {
	return nodeCall(ctx, other, name, argument, 5000, codecA, codecB, where)
}

// nodeCast runs a served function without waiting for it.
func nodeCast[A any](ctx *actor.Context, other string, name actor.Atom, argument A, codecA node.Codec[A], where string) bool {
	result := make(chan bool, 1)
	actor.Spawn(ctx, func(w *actor.Context, _ *actor.PID[struct{}]) int64 {
		result <- isOk[struct{}, string](nodeCall(w, other, name, argument, 30000, codecA, node.Codec[struct{}]{Type: "tuple[]", Encode: func(*node.Encoder, struct{}) {}, Decode: func(*node.Decoder) struct{} { return struct{}{} }}, where))
		return 0
	}, 1, where)
	return true
}

// nodeRegisterGlobal registers a process under a name no connected node has
// for the same message type.
func nodeRegisterGlobal[A any](ctx *actor.Context, p *actor.PID[A], name actor.Atom, codec node.Codec[A]) bool {
	if isSome[*actor.PID[A]](nodeWhereisGlobal(ctx, name, codec)) {
		return false
	}
	return node.Register(ctx, p, string(name), codec)
}

// nodeWhereisGlobal looks for a name on this node, then on each connected
// one.
func nodeWhereisGlobal[A any](ctx *actor.Context, name actor.Atom, codec node.Codec[A]) Toption[*actor.PID[A]] {
	if found, ok := node.Whereis(ctx, string(name), codec); ok {
		return someOf(found)
	}
	for _, other := range node.List(ctx) {
		if found, ok := node.RemoteWhereis(ctx, other, string(name), 2000, codec); ok {
			return someOf(found)
		}
	}
	return noneOf[*actor.PID[A]]()
}

func isSome[A any](o Toption[A]) bool {
	_, ok := o.(Toption_c1[A])
	return ok
}

	names := node.List(ctx)
	slices.Sort(names)
	return mk(names)
}

func registryStart[K comparable, V any](ctx *actor.Context, options L[pair[actor.Atom, actor.Atom]], where string) *actor.Registry[K, V] {
	return registryStartPartitioned[K, V](ctx, options, L[pair[actor.Atom, int64]]{}, where)
}

func registryStartPartitioned[K comparable, V any](ctx *actor.Context, options L[pair[actor.Atom, actor.Atom]], numbers L[pair[actor.Atom, int64]], where string) *actor.Registry[K, V] {
	unique := true
	given := false
	for _, option := range fwd(options) {
		switch {
		case option.E0 == "keys" && option.E1 == "unique":
			unique, given = true, true
		case option.E0 == "keys" && option.E1 == "duplicate":
			unique, given = false, true
		case option.E0 == "keys":
			failAt(ctx, where, "keys: is :unique or :duplicate, not :"+string(option.E1))
		default:
			failAt(ctx, where, "unknown option :"+string(option.E0)+"; Registry.start_link takes keys:")
		}
	}
	if !given {
		failAt(ctx, where, "Registry.start_link needs keys: :unique or keys: :duplicate")
	}
	partitions := int64(1)
	for _, option := range fwd(numbers) {
		switch {
		case option.E0 == "partitions" && option.E1 >= 1 && option.E1 <= 1024:
			partitions = option.E1
		case option.E0 == "partitions":
			failAt(ctx, where, "partitions: is from 1 to 1024")
		default:
			failAt(ctx, where, "unknown option :"+string(option.E0)+"; the numbers Registry.start_link takes are partitions:")
		}
	}
	return actor.NewPartitionedRegistry[K, V](unique, int(partitions))
}

func registryRegister[K comparable, V any](ctx *actor.Context, g *actor.Registry[K, V], key K, value V) Tresult[struct{}, string] {
	if g.Register(ctx, key, value) {
		return okOf(struct{}{})
	}
	return errOf[struct{}]("already_registered")
}

func registryUnregister[K comparable, V any](ctx *actor.Context, g *actor.Registry[K, V], key K) bool {
	return g.Unregister(ctx, key)
}

func registryUnregisterMatch[K comparable, V any](ctx *actor.Context, g *actor.Registry[K, V], key K, match func(*actor.Context, V) bool) int64 {
	return g.UnregisterMatch(ctx, key, func(v V) bool { return match(ctx, v) })
}

func registryLookup[K comparable, V any](g *actor.Registry[K, V], key K) L[V] { return mk(g.Lookup(key)) }

// registryPairs gives entries as {process id, value} tuples.
func registryPairs[K comparable, V any](entries []actor.RegistryEntry[K, V]) L[pair[int64, V]] {
	out := make([]pair[int64, V], len(entries))
	for i, entry := range entries {
		out[i] = pair[int64, V]{entry.ID, entry.Value}
	}
	return mk(out)
}

func registryEntries[K comparable, V any](g *actor.Registry[K, V], key K) L[pair[int64, V]] {
	return registryPairs(g.Entries(key))
}

func registryMatch[K comparable, V any](ctx *actor.Context, g *actor.Registry[K, V], key K, match func(*actor.Context, V) bool) L[pair[int64, V]] {
	var kept []actor.RegistryEntry[K, V]
	for _, entry := range g.Entries(key) {
		if match(ctx, entry.Value) {
			kept = append(kept, entry)
		}
	}
	return registryPairs(kept)
}

func registryCountMatch[K comparable, V any](ctx *actor.Context, g *actor.Registry[K, V], key K, match func(*actor.Context, V) bool) int64 {
	return int64(len(registryMatch(ctx, g, key, match).S))
}

func registryValues[K comparable, V, A any](ctx *actor.Context, g *actor.Registry[K, V], key K, p *actor.PID[A], where string) L[V] {
	return mk(g.Values(key, actor.OwnerOf(ctx, p, where)))
}

func registryDispatch[K comparable, V, B any](ctx *actor.Context, g *actor.Registry[K, V], key K, run func(*actor.Context, L[pair[int64, V]]) B) actor.Atom {
	if entries := g.Entries(key); len(entries) > 0 {
		run(ctx, registryPairs(entries))
	}
	return "ok"
}

func registrySelect[K comparable, V any](ctx *actor.Context, g *actor.Registry[K, V], match func(*actor.Context, K, int64, V) bool) L[triple[K, int64, V]] {
	var out []triple[K, int64, V]
	for _, entry := range g.All() {
		if match(ctx, entry.Key, entry.ID, entry.Value) {
			out = append(out, triple[K, int64, V]{entry.Key, entry.ID, entry.Value})
		}
	}
	return mk(out)
}

func registryUpdateValue[K comparable, V any](ctx *actor.Context, g *actor.Registry[K, V], key K, update func(*actor.Context, V) V) Tresult[pair[V, V], string] {
	value, old, ok := g.UpdateValue(ctx, key, func(v V) V { return update(ctx, v) })
	if !ok {
		return errOf[pair[V, V]]("not_registered")
	}
	return okOf(pair[V, V]{value, old})
}

func registryKeys[K comparable, V, A any](ctx *actor.Context, g *actor.Registry[K, V], p *actor.PID[A], where string) L[K] {
	return mk(g.Keys(actor.OwnerOf(ctx, p, where)))
}

func registryCount[K comparable, V any](g *actor.Registry[K, V]) int64 { return g.Count() }

func registryPartitions[K comparable, V any](g *actor.Registry[K, V]) int64 { return int64(g.Partitions()) }

func registryPutMeta[K comparable, V, M any](g *actor.Registry[K, V], name actor.Atom, value M) actor.Atom {
	g.PutMeta(string(name), value)
	return "ok"
}

func registryMeta[K comparable, V, M any](g *actor.Registry[K, V], name actor.Atom) Toption[M] {
	if value, ok := g.Meta(string(name)); ok {
		if typed, fits := value.(M); fits {
			return someOf(typed)
		}
	}
	return noneOf[M]()
}

func dynamicStart(ctx *actor.Context, where string) *actor.DynamicSupervisor {
	return actor.StartDynamicSupervisor(ctx, 3, 5000, where)
}

func dynamicStartOptions(ctx *actor.Context, options L[pair[actor.Atom, int64]], where string) *actor.DynamicSupervisor {
	limit, seconds, most := int64(3), int64(5), int64(0)
	for _, option := range fwd(options) {
		switch option.E0 {
		case "max_restarts":
			limit = option.E1
		case "max_seconds":
			seconds = option.E1
		case "max_children":
			if option.E1 < 0 {
				failAt(ctx, where, "max_children: cannot be negative")
			}
			most = option.E1
		default:
			failAt(ctx, where, "unknown option :"+string(option.E0)+"; DynamicSupervisor.start_link takes max_restarts:, max_seconds: and max_children:")
		}
	}
	s := actor.StartDynamicSupervisor(ctx, limit, seconds*1000, where)
	s.LimitChildren(most)
	return s
}

func dynamicStartChild[A any](ctx *actor.Context, s *actor.DynamicSupervisor, body func(*actor.Context, *actor.PID[A]) int64, where string) Tresult[*actor.PID[A], string] {
	return dynamicStartChildAs(ctx, s, body, "permanent", where)
}

func dynamicStartChildAs[A any](ctx *actor.Context, s *actor.DynamicSupervisor, body func(*actor.Context, *actor.PID[A]) int64, restart actor.Atom, where string) Tresult[*actor.PID[A], string] {
	p, problem := actor.StartDynamicChild(ctx, s, body, string(restart), where)
	if problem != "" {
		return errOf[*actor.PID[A]](problem)
	}
	return okOf(p)
}

func dynamicStartServer[S, Q, R any](ctx *actor.Context, s *actor.DynamicSupervisor, state S, handle func(*actor.Context, S, Q) struct {
	E0 S
	E1 R
}, where string) Tresult[*actor.Server[Q, R], string] {
	server, problem := actor.StartDynamicServer(ctx, s, state, handle, where)
	if problem != "" {
		return errOf[*actor.Server[Q, R]](problem)
	}
	return okOf(server)
}

func dynamicTerminate[A any](ctx *actor.Context, s *actor.DynamicSupervisor, p *actor.PID[A], where string) bool {
	if actor.RemoteOf(p) != nil {
		return false
	}
	return actor.TerminateDynamicChild(ctx, s, actor.LocalID(p), where)
}

func dynamicChildren(ctx *actor.Context, s *actor.DynamicSupervisor, where string) L[int64] {
	return mk(actor.DynamicChildren(ctx, s, where))
}

func dynamicCount(ctx *actor.Context, s *actor.DynamicSupervisor, where string) int64 {
	return int64(len(actor.DynamicChildren(ctx, s, where)))
}

func dynamicRestarts(ctx *actor.Context, s *actor.DynamicSupervisor, where string) int64 {
	return actor.DynamicRestarts(ctx, s, where)
}

func dynamicStop(ctx *actor.Context, s *actor.DynamicSupervisor, where string) bool {
	return actor.StopDynamicSupervisor(ctx, s, 5000, where)
}

func dynamicAlive(ctx *actor.Context, s *actor.DynamicSupervisor, where string) bool {
	return actor.DynamicSupervisorAlive(ctx, s, where)
}

// ---- TCP ----

// socketHandle is a TCP listener or connection. A connection reads through
// a buffer, so lines and byte counts can be mixed.
type socketHandle struct {
	mu       sync.Mutex
	listener net.Listener
	conn     net.Conn
	reader   *bufio.Reader
	closed   bool
	// owner is the process whose end closes the socket.
	owner *actor.Context
	// receiver is the controlling process given by controlling_process,
	// which an active socket sends its events to; without one, they go to
	// the owner when its messages are events.
	receiver any
	// packet is how the stream is cut into packets: raw, line, or a length
	// header of 1, 2 or 4 bytes. partial holds a packet read in part.
	packet  string
	partial []byte
	// active counts the packets still to be sent as messages: -1 for all of
	// them, 0 when the socket is passive. reading is set while a goroutine
	// sends them, and reads holds a read in progress, active or passive.
	active  int64
	reading bool
	reads   sync.Mutex
	// datagrams is the connection of a UDP socket.
	datagrams net.PacketConn
	// inherit holds the options of a listener, for the sockets it accepts.
	inherit L[pair[actor.Atom, actor.Atom]]
}

// own makes a process the socket's owner, closing the socket when it ends.
func (s *socketHandle) own(owner *actor.Context) {
	s.mu.Lock()
	s.owner = owner
	s.mu.Unlock()
	actor.OnEnd(owner, func() {
		s.mu.Lock()
		current := s.owner == owner
		s.mu.Unlock()
		if current {
			s.close()
		}
	})
}

// netSlice is how long a blocking network call waits before checking
// whether its process has been asked to stop.
const netSlice = 50 * time.Millisecond

// netDeadline is when a call that may wait ms milliseconds gives up; a
// negative wait has none.
func netDeadline(ms int64) (time.Time, bool) {
	if ms < 0 {
		return time.Time{}, false
	}
	return time.Now().Add(time.Duration(ms) * time.Millisecond), true
}

// netWait runs attempt in slices until it succeeds, fails for a reason other
// than a timeout, the deadline passes or the process is asked to stop.
func netWait(ctx *actor.Context, ms int64, setDeadline func(time.Time) error, attempt func() error) error {
	until, bounded := netDeadline(ms)
	for {
		actor.Check(ctx)
		slice := time.Now().Add(netSlice)
		if bounded && until.Before(slice) {
			slice = until
		}
		setDeadline(slice)
		err := attempt()
		if err == nil {
			return nil
		}
		var timeout net.Error
		if !errors.As(err, &timeout) || !timeout.Timeout() {
			return err
		}
		if bounded && !time.Now().Before(until) {
			return err
		}
	}
}

func netProblem(err error) string {
	var timeout net.Error
	switch {
	case errors.As(err, &timeout) && timeout.Timeout():
		return "timeout"
	case errors.Is(err, io.EOF), errors.Is(err, net.ErrClosed):
		return "closed"
	}
	return err.Error()
}

func tcpListen(ctx *actor.Context, address string) Tresult[*socketHandle, string] {
	listener, err := net.Listen("tcp", address)
	if err != nil {
		return errOf[*socketHandle](err.Error())
	}
	s := &socketHandle{listener: listener}
	s.own(ctx)
	return okOf(s)
}

// tcpListenWith listens with options that the sockets it accepts inherit.
func tcpListenWith(ctx *actor.Context, address string, options L[pair[actor.Atom, actor.Atom]], where string) Tresult[*socketHandle, string] {
	listening := tcpListen(ctx, address)
	if s, ok := listening.(Tresult_c0[*socketHandle, string]); ok {
		s.F0.inherit = tcpOptions(ctx, options, "TCP.listen", where)
	}
	return listening
}

// tcpConnectWith connects and sets options on the new socket.
